     │ ---                                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Requirements: explicit vs. assumed                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Explicit (README):                                                      │
     │ - Exactly two endpoints:                                                │
     │   - (a) update the balances of all deposits;                            │
     │   - (b) get all deposits as id, planType, balance, days, withdrawals.   │
     │ - Tables:                                                               │
     │   - timeDeposits(id int PK, planType string req, days int req, balance  │
     │     decimal req);                                                       │
     │   - withdrawals(id int PK, timeDepositId int FK req, amount decimal     │
     │     req, date date req).                                                │
     │ - Keep the TimeDeposit API and the updateBalance signature and          │
     │   behavior.                                                             │
     │ - Use a Swagger/OpenAPI contract, with instructions for calling the     │
     │   endpoints through Swagger.                                            │
     │ - Invalid input and exceptions need not be handled.                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Assumed (documented in §9): HTTP verbs and paths, status codes, JSON    │
     │ shape of withdrawals, decimal precision, quoted camelCase names, demo   │
     │ seed data, ordering, concurrency, and that Swagger's own documentation  │
     │ endpoints don't count as API endpoints.                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 2. REST contract                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ┌──────────────────────┬─────────┬─────────┬──────────────────────┐     │
     │ │       Endpoint       │ Request │ Success │         Body         │     │
     │ ├──────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────┼──────────────────────┤     │
     │ │ POST /time-deposits/ │ none    │ 204 No  │ none                 │     │
     │ │ balance-updates      │         │ Content │                      │     │
     │ ├──────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────┼──────────────────────┤     │
     │ │ GET /time-deposits   │ none    │ 200 OK  │ TimeDepositResponse[ │     │
     │ │                      │         │         │ ]                    │     │
     │ └──────────────────────┴─────────┴─────────┴──────────────────────┘     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Response DTOs (Java records in the web adapter):                      │
     │   - TimeDepositResponse(int id, String planType, BigDecimal balance,    │
     │     int days, List<WithdrawalResponse> withdrawals)                     │
     │   - WithdrawalResponse(int id, BigDecimal amount, LocalDate date) →     │
     │     JSON "date": "2026-01-15".                                          │
     │ - Errors: Spring's default 500, with no custom error handling (README). │
     │ - Swagger: UI at /swagger-ui.html, JSON at /v3/api-docs, generated      │
     │   code-first from @Operation / @ApiResponse annotations on the          │
     │   controller.                                                           │
     │ - No request DTOs: neither endpoint takes input.                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 3. Responsibilities                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Part: Domain: TimeDeposit, PlanType, TimeDepositCalculator              │
     │   (unchanged); new Withdrawal record                                    │
     │ Responsibility: Interest rules; withdrawal value (BigDecimal            │
     │   amount, LocalDate date)                                               │
     │ Framework-free?: Yes                                                    │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Application: TimeDepositService                                   │
     │ Responsibility: Two use cases: updateAllBalances() (load →              │
     │   calculate → save) and getAllTimeDeposits(); owns the transactions     │
     │ Framework-free?: Spring only for @Service / @Transactional              │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Output port: TimeDepositRepository (interface, application        │
     │   package)                                                              │
     │ Responsibility: What the application needs from storage                 │
     │ Framework-free?: Yes                                                    │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Input adapter: TimeDepositController + response records           │
     │ Responsibility: HTTP ↔ use case; maps domain → DTO; OpenAPI             │
     │   annotations                                                           │
     │ Framework-free?: No                                                     │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Persistence adapter: entities, Spring Data repository,            │
     │   TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter implements TimeDepositRepository        │
     │ Responsibility: JPA mapping; entity ↔ domain; double ↔ NUMERIC          │
     │   conversion                                                            │
     │ Framework-free?: No                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Interfaces and types I'm deliberately leaving out:                      │
     │ - No input-port interfaces: the controller calls the concrete service.  │
     │ - No separate interface per use case: two methods on one service.       │
     │ - No mapper framework: mapping is a few lines in each adapter.          │
     │ - The only interface is the output port. It keeps the application and   │
     │   domain independent of JPA, and it can be replaced by a fake in a unit │
     │   test.                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 4. Packages and dependency direction                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ org.ikigaidigital                        ← domain (fixed: shared FQNs)  │
     │ + TimeDepositApplication (@SpringBootApplication, @Bean                 │
     │ TimeDepositCalculator)                                                  │
     │   TimeDeposit, TimeDepositCalculator, PlanType, Withdrawal              │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.application            ← TimeDepositService,          │
     │ TimeDepositRepository (port), TimeDepositWithWithdrawals (read record)  │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.adapter.in.web         ← TimeDepositController,       │
     │ TimeDepositResponse, WithdrawalResponse                                 │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.adapter.out.persistence← TimeDepositEntity,           │
     │ WithdrawalEntity, TimeDepositJpaRepository,                             │
     │ TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter                                           │
     │ - Direction: adapter.* → application → domain. The domain imports       │
     │   nothing from the other layers, and the application imports no         │
     │   adapter.                                                              │
     │ - Calculator bean: TimeDepositCalculator stays free of annotations; the │
     │   main class declares it as a @Bean.                                    │
     │ - Read model: TimeDepositWithWithdrawals(TimeDeposit deposit,           │
     │   List<Withdrawal> withdrawals) combines a deposit with its withdrawals │
     │   without repeating the deposit's fields.                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Database, mapping, transactions                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Flyway migrations:                                                      │
     │ - V1__create_tables.sql:                                                │
     │ CREATE TABLE "timeDeposits" (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, "planType"         │
     │ VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,                                                   │
     │                              days INTEGER NOT NULL, balance             │
     │ NUMERIC(19,2) NOT NULL);                                                │
     │ CREATE TABLE withdrawals (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,                       │
     │                           "timeDepositId" INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES   │
     │ "timeDeposits"(id),                                                     │
     │                           amount NUMERIC(19,2) NOT NULL, date DATE NOT  │
     │ NULL);                                                                  │
     │ CREATE INDEX withdrawals_time_deposit_id_idx ON                         │
     │ withdrawals("timeDepositId");                                           │
     │   - No CHECK constraint on planType: unknown plans must stay storable   │
     │     and earn 0 (legacy behavior).                                       │
     │   - No ID generation: there is no create endpoint.                      │
     │ - V2__seed_demo_data.sql: a few deposits covering each plan's           │
     │   boundaries (basic 31, student 366, premium 45/46, an unknown plan)    │
     │   plus withdrawals, so Swagger shows real data straight away.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ JPA:                                                                    │
     │ - Naming: spring.jpa.hibernate.naming.physical-strategy=PhysicalNamingS │
     │   trategyStandardImpl and hibernate.globally_quoted_identifiers=true,   │
     │   so @Table(name = "timeDeposits") and @Column(name = "planType") map   │
     │   exactly. Spring Boot's default strategy would turn them into          │
     │   snake_case.                                                           │
     │ - Validation: ddl-auto=validate, so Flyway owns the schema.             │
     │ - spring.jpa.open-in-view=false.                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Entities:                                                               │
     │ - TimeDepositEntity holds @OneToMany(mappedBy) List<WithdrawalEntity>   │
     │   withdrawals, and WithdrawalEntity has a @ManyToOne back to it.        │
     │ - Balance and amount are BigDecimal.                                    │
     │ - The read query uses one fetch join, select distinct d from            │
     │   TimeDepositEntity d left join fetch d.withdrawals order by d.id, so   │
     │   withdrawals don't cost one query per deposit (the N+1 problem).       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Converting between the database and the domain (the only place where    │
     │ precision changes):                                                     │
     │ - Loading: entity.getBalance().doubleValue(). A 2-decimal value up to   │
     │   about 9e13 converts to a double exactly enough to round-trip.         │
     │ - Saving: BigDecimal.valueOf(deposit.getBalance()).setScale(2,          │
     │   HALF_UP).                                                             │
     │   - This rounds away the double noise the calculator can produce (e.g.  │
     │     1237654.3099999998 → 1237654.31) explicitly, instead of leaving it  │
     │     to the database.                                                    │
     │   - The calculator's output is unchanged; this is storage formatting    │
     │     only.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Port (TimeDepositRepository):                                           │
     │ - List<TimeDeposit> findAllForUpdate()                                  │
     │ - List<TimeDepositWithWithdrawals> findAllWithWithdrawals()             │
     │ - void saveBalances(List<TimeDeposit> deposits)                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Transactions:                                                           │
     │ - updateAllBalances() runs as one @Transactional: load all →            │
     │   calculator.updateBalance(deposits) → saveBalances. It is all or       │
     │   nothing.                                                              │
     │ - findAllForUpdate uses @Lock(PESSIMISTIC_WRITE) (SELECT … FOR UPDATE). │
     │   Concurrent POSTs are handled one after the other instead of losing    │
     │   an update. It's one annotation and needs no schema change.            │
     │   (Recommended; can be dropped to "document only" — see §9.)            │
     │ - getAllTimeDeposits() runs as @Transactional(readOnly = true).         │
     │ - saveBalances: findAllById(ids) returns the entities already loaded in │
     │   this transaction; their balances are set and Hibernate writes them    │
     │   on commit.                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. Reusing TimeDepositCalculator unchanged                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - The service calls calculator.updateBalance(List<TimeDeposit>) exactly │
     │   as it is.                                                             │
     │ - The adapter builds TimeDeposit objects with the public constructor    │
     │   new TimeDeposit(id, planType, balance.doubleValue(), days) and reads  │
     │   them back with getBalance().                                          │
     │ - The interest is calculated in double as before; BigDecimal exists     │
     │   only at the database and JSON boundaries.                             │
     │ - None of TimeDeposit, PlanType, TimeDepositCalculator or the existing  │
     │   tests change. Step 1 checks this with git diff --quiet on those       │
     │   paths.                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 7. Test strategy                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Level: Unit (existing)                                                  │
     │ Test: TimeDepositCalculatorTest, PlanTypeTest: unchanged                │
     │ Proves: Domain behavior                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Level: Unit                                                             │
     │ Test: TimeDepositServiceTest with a hand-written in-memory fake of      │
     │   the port (no Mockito)                                                 │
     │ Proves: update = calculate + save of every deposit; GET returns         │
     │   what the port returns                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Level: Web slice                                                        │
     │ Test: @WebMvcTest(TimeDepositController) + @MockitoBean service         │
     │ Proves: Paths, 204 / 200, exact JSON field names and the                │
     │   withdrawals shape                                                     │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Level: Persistence                                                      │
     │ Test: @DataJpaTest + @AutoConfigureTestDatabase(replace = NONE) +       │
     │   Testcontainers PostgreSQL + Flyway                                    │
     │ Proves: Migrations apply; quoted camelCase mapping validates;           │
     │   withdrawals load in one query; decimal round trip, including          │
     │   1237654.3099999998 → 1237654.31; FK                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Level: End-to-end                                                       │
     │ Test: @SpringBootTest(RANDOM_PORT) + Testcontainers; @Sql sets a        │
     │   known fixture                                                         │
     │ Proves: POST → 204; GET → expected balances (10008.33, 10041.67,        │
     │   unchanged for student day 366 and premium day 45, unknown plan        │
     │   unchanged); a second POST compounds (10016.67)                        │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Level: Contract                                                         │
     │ Test: Same context: GET /v3/api-docs                                    │
     │ Proves: Exactly the two API paths are documented                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Shared container: one TestcontainersConfiguration                     │
     │   (@TestConfiguration, @Bean @ServiceConnection PostgreSQLContainer),   │
     │   imported by the database tests.                                       │
     │ - Requirement: the database tests need Docker running.                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 8. Implementation sequence                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Each step is one commit, and mvn test stays green after each.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Spring Boot skeleton:                                                │
     │    - pom.xml: parent spring-boot-starter-parent:3.5.16.                 │
     │    - Starters: web, data-jpa, flyway-core + flyway-database-postgresql, │
     │      postgresql (runtime), springdoc-openapi-starter-webmvc-ui (a 2.x   │
     │      version compatible with Boot 3.5, confirmed by the step-5 contract │
     │      test).                                                             │
     │    - Test dependencies: spring-boot-starter-test,                       │
     │      spring-boot-testcontainers, testcontainers postgresql +            │
     │      junit-jupiter.                                                     │
     │    - Remove the explicit JUnit, AssertJ and surefire versions; the Boot │
     │      BOM manages them.                                                  │
     │    - Add TimeDepositApplication and TimeDepositCalculator as a @Bean.   │
     │    - Check: the 53 existing tests pass, plus a context-loads test.      │
     │ 2. Schema: Flyway V1, V2 seed, application.properties (datasource with  │
     │    environment-variable overrides, JPA settings from §5), and           │
     │    compose.yaml with PostgreSQL for local runs.                         │
     │    - Check: a Testcontainers context test confirms the migrations       │
     │      apply.                                                             │
     │ 3. Persistence: domain Withdrawal; entities, TimeDepositJpaRepository,  │
     │    port, TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter.                                 │
     │    - Check: the @DataJpaTest suite.                                     │
     │ 4. Application: TimeDepositService with transactions and the lock.      │
     │    - Check: TimeDepositServiceTest, using the fake port.                │
     │ 5. Web: controller, response records and OpenAPI annotations.           │
     │    - Check: the @WebMvcTest, end-to-end and /v3/api-docs tests.         │
     │ 6. Documentation:                                                       │
     │    - README: run instructions (docker compose up -d, mvn                │
     │      spring-boot:run, Swagger UI URL, "Try it out" steps for both       │
     │      endpoints) and the assumptions;                                    │
     │    - update docs/AI_USAGE.md and docs/INITIAL_ANALYSIS.md;              │
     │    - dependency and dead-code review.                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 9. Ambiguities to document (README, INITIAL_ANALYSIS.md, comments where │
     │ applied)                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Endpoint verbs and paths: POST /time-deposits/balance-updates → 204  │
     │    and GET /time-deposits → 200. The README specifies neither.          │
     │ 2. "Exactly two endpoints": springdoc's /v3/api-docs and /swagger-ui    │
     │    are taken to be documentation, not API endpoints, since the README   │
     │    requires Swagger.                                                    │
     │ 3. Repeated POSTs: each call applies another month and days doesn't     │
     │    change (the legacy calculator). Not idempotent by design;            │
     │    documented, not guarded.                                             │
     │ 4. Concurrent POSTs: serialized with a pessimistic lock (§5). This is   │
     │    the one design addition beyond the README; the alternative is to     │
     │    document the race only.                                              │
     │ 5. Precision: NUMERIC(19,2). double noise is rounded HALF_UP to 2       │
     │    decimals when saving. Balances above about 9e13 lose double          │
     │    precision, which is the legacy limit.                                │
     │ 6. Table and column names: camelCase kept exactly via quoted            │
     │    identifiers.                                                         │
     │ 7. Plan types: not constrained in the database. Unknown types are       │
     │    stored and earn 0.                                                   │
     │ 8. withdrawals in the response: an array of {id, amount, date}, ordered │
     │    by id, and [] when there are none. Withdrawals don't affect          │
     │    interest.                                                            │
     │ 9. Order of deposits: ordered by id.                                    │
     │ 10. IDs: plain INTEGER primary keys with no generation, because no      │
     │     endpoint creates records. Seed and test data set the IDs            │
     │     explicitly.                                                         │
     │ 11. Seed data: demo rows in V2 make the API usable out of the box.      │
     │     Tests replace them with their own fixtures.                         │
     │ 12. Who increments days: out of scope. days is stored data.             │
     │ 13. Dates: LocalDate with no time zone, as ISO strings in JSON.         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verification (whole plan)                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - After every step:                                                     │
     │   - mvn test is green;                                                  │
     │   - git diff --quiet HEAD~N -- src/main/java/org/ikigaidigital/{TimeDep │
     │     osit,TimeDepositCalculator,PlanType}.java                           │
     │     src/test/java/org/ikigaidigital/ shows the domain and existing      │
     │     tests unchanged.                                                    │
     │ - At the end: docker compose up -d && mvn spring-boot:run, then in      │
     │   Swagger UI call GET → POST → GET and check the balances against the   │
     │   seed values.                                                          │
     │ - Prerequisite: start Docker Desktop before steps 2–5.                  │
     ╰─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯

❯ The overall architecture plan is approved in principle, but please revise a   
  few details before implementation.                                            
                                                                                
  1. Spring Boot bootstrap: account for DataSource/Flyway auto-configuration    
  when adding JPA dependencies. Do not introduce a context-loads test that      
  requires PostgreSQL before the Testcontainers setup exists.                   
  2. Persistence: review whether findAllForUpdate() followed by saveBalances()  
  and findAllById() causes unnecessary queries. Keep domain/JPA separation, but 
  choose the simplest correct persistence workflow.                             
  3. Concurrency: retain pessimistic locking if justified, ensure deterministic 
  lock ordering, and document the distinction between concurrency safety and    
  idempotency.                                                                  
  4. Flyway: keep schema migrations separate from demo seed data. Make demo     
  data available only in a development/test context, not in every environment.  
  5. Money: document and test the BigDecimal/double persistence boundary,       
  including the fact that the stored two-decimal value may differ from the      
  calculator's raw double representation.                                       
  6. Testing: review the proposed test layers for redundancy. Prefer a focused  
  suite with meaningful integration tests over unnecessary mocks and            
  overlapping tests.                                                            
                                                                                
  Preserve all confirmed README requirements, the existing domain               
  implementation and characterization tests.                                    
                                                                                
  Update the implementation plan with your recommended solutions and            
  trade-offs. Do not modify application code yet.

     │ Architecture & implementation plan: Spring Boot, REST API, persistence  │

     │ - TimeDeposit (shared, String planType, Double balance);                │
     │ - PlanType (enum: each plan's code, rate and interest window);          │
     │ - TimeDepositCalculator.updateBalance(List<TimeDeposit>).               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ It is covered by 43 characterization tests plus 10 PlanTypeTest tests.  │
     │ Next comes what the README asks for: two REST endpoints and a           │
     │ PostgreSQL database with timeDeposits and withdrawals, using a          │
     │ pragmatic hexagonal structure. The domain code and the existing tests   │
     │ stay unchanged.                                                         │
     │                                                                         │

     │ count, lock ordering, separating demo data, the money boundary, and     │
     │ test redundancy.                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Repository facts that shape the plan:                                   │
     │ - plain Maven, no wrapper;                                              │
     │ - Java 17 target; the JDK is 21;                                        │
     │ - Docker is installed but not running; it must be started before step   │
     │   2;                                                                    │
     │ - latest Spring Boot 3.x on Maven Central: 3.5.16 (checked).            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │

     │     decimal req);                                                       │
     │   - withdrawals(id int PK, timeDepositId int FK req, amount decimal     │
     │     req, date date req).                                                │
     │ - Keep the TimeDeposit API and the updateBalance signature and          │
     │   behavior.                                                             │
     │ - Use a Swagger/OpenAPI contract, with instructions for calling the     │
     │   endpoints through Swagger.                                            │
     │ - Invalid input and exceptions need not be handled.                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Assumed: everything in §9.                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 2. REST contract                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ┌──────────────────────┬─────────┬─────────┬──────────────────────┐     │
     │ │       Endpoint       │ Request │ Success │         Body         │     │
     │ ├──────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────┼──────────────────────┤     │
     │ │ POST /time-deposits/ │ none    │ 204 No  │ none                 │     │
     │ │ balance-updates      │         │ Content │                      │     │
     │ ├──────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────┼──────────────────────┤     │
     │ │ GET /time-deposits   │ none    │ 200 OK  │ TimeDepositResponse[ │     │
     │ │                      │         │         │ ], ordered by id     │     │
     │ └──────────────────────┴─────────┴─────────┴──────────────────────┘     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Response DTOs (records in the web adapter):                           │
     │   - TimeDepositResponse(int id, String planType, BigDecimal balance,    │
     │     int days, List<WithdrawalResponse> withdrawals);                    │
     │   - WithdrawalResponse(int id, BigDecimal amount, LocalDate date).      │
     │ - JSON values: money is a number with two decimals (10008.33,           │
     │   10000.00); dates are ISO strings ("2026-01-15").                      │
     │ - Errors: Spring's default 500, with no custom error handling (README). │
     │   There are no request DTOs, because neither endpoint takes input.      │
     │ - Swagger: UI at /swagger-ui.html, JSON at /v3/api-docs, generated      │
     │   code-first from @Operation / @ApiResponse annotations.                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 3. Responsibilities                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Part: Domain (unchanged: TimeDeposit, PlanType,                         │
     │   TimeDepositCalculator; new: Withdrawal record with BigDecimal         │
     │   amount and LocalDate date)                                            │
     │ Responsibility: Interest rules and domain values. No framework          │
     │   dependencies.                                                         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Application: TimeDepositService                                   │
     │ Responsibility: updateAllBalances() (load locked → calculate →          │
     │   save) and getAllTimeDeposits(). Owns the transactions.                │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Output port: TimeDepositRepository (interface in the              │
     │   application package)                                                  │
     │ Responsibility: The storage operations the application needs. The       │
     │   only interface in the design: it keeps the application and domain     │
     │   independent of JPA.                                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Input adapter: TimeDepositController + response records           │
     │ Responsibility: HTTP ↔ use case; domain → DTO mapping; OpenAPI          │
     │   annotations.                                                          │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Part: Persistence adapter: entities, TimeDepositJpaRepository,          │
     │   TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter                                         │
     │ Responsibility: JPA mapping, entity ↔ domain conversion, the money      │
     │   boundary (§5.4).                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Deliberately left out: input-port interfaces, one interface per use     │
     │ case, mapper frameworks, and request DTOs.                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 4. Packages and dependency direction                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ org.ikigaidigital                         ← domain (shared FQNs, fixed) │
     │ + TimeDepositApplication (@SpringBootApplication, @Bean                 │
     │ TimeDepositCalculator)                                                  │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.application             ← TimeDepositService,         │
     │ TimeDepositRepository (port), TimeDepositWithWithdrawals (read record)  │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.adapter.in.web          ← TimeDepositController,      │
     │ TimeDepositResponse, WithdrawalResponse                                 │
     │ org.ikigaidigital.adapter.out.persistence ← TimeDepositEntity,          │
     │ WithdrawalEntity, TimeDepositJpaRepository,                             │
     │ TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter                                           │
     │ - Direction: adapter.* → application → domain.                          │
     │ - Calculator bean: TimeDepositCalculator stays free of annotations; the │
     │   main class declares it as a @Bean.                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Persistence                                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5.1 Schema migrations and demo data, kept separate                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Schema: src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__create_tables.sql,        │
     │   applied in every environment.                                         │
     │ CREATE TABLE "timeDeposits" (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, "planType"         │
     │ VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,                                                   │
     │                              days INTEGER NOT NULL, balance             │
     │ NUMERIC(19,2) NOT NULL);                                                │
     │ CREATE TABLE withdrawals (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,                       │
     │                           "timeDepositId" INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES   │
     │ "timeDeposits"(id),                                                     │
     │                           amount NUMERIC(19,2) NOT NULL, date DATE NOT  │
     │ NULL);                                                                  │
     │ CREATE INDEX withdrawals_time_deposit_id_idx ON                         │
     │ withdrawals("timeDepositId");                                           │
     │   - No CHECK constraint on planType: unknown plans stay storable and    │
     │     earn 0.                                                             │
     │   - No ID generation: there is no create endpoint.                      │
     │ - Demo data: src/main/resources/db/demo/R__demo_data.sql.               │
     │   - A Flyway repeatable migration with INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO     │
     │     NOTHING, so it is safe to re-run.                                   │
     │   - Enabled only by the dev profile: application-dev.properties sets    │
     │     spring.flyway.locations=classpath:db/migration,classpath:db/demo.   │
     │   - The default configuration applies only db/migration. Integration    │
     │     tests use their own @Sql fixtures, not the demo rows.               │
     │   - Repeatable rather than numbered, so it never takes a schema version │
     │     number or blocks later V2__… schema migrations.                     │
     │   - Trade-off: when the demo script changes, Flyway re-runs it, but DO  │
     │     NOTHING won't update rows that already exist. That's acceptable for │
     │     demo data; reset with docker compose down -v.                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5.2 JPA mapping                                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Naming: spring.jpa.hibernate.naming.physical-strategy=org.hibernate.b │
     │   oot.model.naming.PhysicalNamingStrategyStandardImpl plus              │
     │   spring.jpa.properties.hibernate.globally_quoted_identifiers=true.     │
     │   - @Table(name = "timeDeposits") and @Column(name = "planType") then   │
     │     map exactly. Spring Boot's default strategy would rewrite them to   │
     │     snake_case.                                                         │
     │ - Schema ownership: ddl-auto=validate, so Flyway owns the schema.       │
     │ - Other settings:                                                       │
     │   - spring.jpa.open-in-view=false;                                      │
     │   - hibernate.jdbc.batch_size=50 and hibernate.order_updates=true, so   │
     │     balance updates are sent in batches.                                │
     │ - Entities:                                                             │
     │   - TimeDepositEntity has a lazy @OneToMany(mappedBy = "timeDeposit")   │
     │     List<WithdrawalEntity>;                                             │
     │   - WithdrawalEntity has a @ManyToOne back;                             │
     │   - money fields are BigDecimal.                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5.3 Simplest correct workflow (query count)                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Rev. 1 problem: findAllForUpdate() → saveBalances() → findAllById() ran │
     │ an extra SELECT … WHERE id IN (…) for entities that were already        │
     │ loaded and locked.                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Rev. 2:                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Port method: List<TimeDeposit> findAllForUpdate()                       │
     │ Adapter implementation: @Lock(PESSIMISTIC_WRITE) @Query("select d       │
     │   from TimeDepositEntity d order by d.id") → map to TimeDeposit         │
     │   (withdrawals not touched, so not loaded)                              │
     │ SQL: 1 × SELECT … ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE                                │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Port method: void saveBalances(List<TimeDeposit>)                       │
     │ Adapter implementation:                                                 │
     │ getReferenceById(id).setBalance(toStoredBalance(…))                     │
     │   for each deposit; Hibernate writes on commit                          │
     │ SQL: 0 × SELECT; ≤ N × UPDATE (only rows that changed), batched         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Port method: List<TimeDepositWithWithdrawals>                           │
     │   findAllWithWithdrawals()                                              │
     │ Adapter implementation: select d from TimeDepositEntity d left join     │
     │   fetch d.withdrawals order by d.id                                     │
     │ SQL: 1 × SELECT (no N+1)                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Why getReferenceById costs no query: it returns the instance that     │
     │   findAllForUpdate already loaded into the same transaction. The        │
     │   service's @Transactional guarantees that. A code comment will state   │
     │   this contract.                                                        │
     │   - If it were ever called outside that transaction, it would still be  │
     │     correct, just slower (lazy loads).                                  │
     │ - Alternative rejected: a callback port method such as                  │
     │   updateAll(Consumer<List<TimeDeposit>>). It avoids relying on the      │
     │   persistence context, but it's a less obvious API for the same result. │
     │ - Why the lock query has no join: PostgreSQL rejects FOR UPDATE on the  │
     │   nullable side of an outer join. The update path doesn't need          │
     │   withdrawals anyway.                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5.4 Money boundary (BigDecimal ↔ double)                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Loading: entity.getBalance().doubleValue(). A 2-decimal value maps to │
     │   the nearest double and back without loss up to about 9×10¹³; beyond   │
     │   that, the legacy double precision applies.                            │
     │ - Saving: toStoredBalance(double) =                                     │
     │   BigDecimal.valueOf(value).setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP). This is  │
     │   explicit; we don't leave it to the database's implicit rounding.      │
     │ - The stored value can differ from the calculator's raw double:         │
     │   - student 1234567.89 → the calculator gives 1237654.3099999998        │
     │     (pinned by the characterization test);                              │
     │   - stored and returned value: 1237654.31;                              │
     │   - the next POST calculates from 1237654.31, not from the double.      │
     │ - Consequence: applying N months through the API can, in rare cases,    │
     │   differ by a cent from calling updateBalance N times in memory. The    │
     │   calculator is unchanged; the README's Decimal column is what imposes  │
     │   2 decimals.                                                           │
     │ - Where it's documented: README, INITIAL_ANALYSIS.md and a comment on   │
     │   toStoredBalance.                                                      │
     │ - Where it's tested: persistence integration test and end-to-end test   │
     │   (§7).                                                                 │
     │ - JSON: the GET path maps entity BigDecimal → TimeDeposit double →      │
     │   BigDecimal.valueOf(double).setScale(2, HALF_UP). For stored values    │
     │   this conversion is exact, and every value has two decimals.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5.5 Transactions and concurrency                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Update: updateAllBalances() is one @Transactional: lock and load →    │
     │   calculator.updateBalance(deposits) → saveBalances. It is all or       │
     │   nothing.                                                              │
     │ - Read: getAllTimeDeposits() is @Transactional(readOnly = true).        │
     │ - Why the pessimistic lock is justified: without it, two POSTs running  │
     │   at the same time both read the same balances, and the last commit     │
     │   wins. Both report 204, but only one month is applied: a lost update   │
     │   on money. With FOR UPDATE, the second transaction waits, then reads   │
     │   the first one's committed balances, so both months are applied        │
     │   correctly.                                                            │
     │   - Cost: one annotation, no schema change.                             │
     │   - Plain SELECTs, such as GET, are not blocked (PostgreSQL MVCC).      │
     │ - Deterministic lock ordering: ORDER BY id together with FOR UPDATE.    │
     │   PostgreSQL sorts the rows before locking them, so every update locks  │
     │   rows in ascending id order, and two updates can't deadlock.           │
     │   - Rejected alternative: optimistic @Version. It needs a column the    │
     │     README schema doesn't have, plus retry logic.                       │
     │ - Concurrency safety is not idempotency (documented separately):        │
     │   - Concurrency safety: POSTs arriving at the same time each apply      │
     │     exactly one month, and none is lost. The lock provides this.        │
     │   - Idempotency: a repeated or retried POST does not have the effect of │
     │     a single call; every POST applies another month. This is            │
     │     intentional, legacy calculator behavior, and it is not guarded:     │
     │     preventing it would need a "last applied" marker or a request key,  │
     │     which aren't in the README.                                         │
     │ - Lock waits use the database default (no timeout). This is documented  │
     │   as a known limitation.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. Reusing TimeDepositCalculator unchanged                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - The service calls calculator.updateBalance(deposits) exactly as it    │
     │   is. The adapter builds deposits with new TimeDeposit(id, planType,    │
     │   balance.doubleValue(), days) and reads them back with getBalance().   │
     │ - BigDecimal exists only at the database and JSON boundaries (§5.4).    │
     │ - None of TimeDeposit, PlanType, TimeDepositCalculator or the existing  │
     │   tests change. Every step checks this with git diff.                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 7. Test suite                                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Rev. 1 had a service unit test with a fake and a @WebMvcTest with       │
     │ mocks. Both overlapped with the end-to-end test: the service is a       │
     │ three-line orchestration, and the JSON shape is better checked against  │
     │ the real stack. Both are dropped. No Mockito anywhere.                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Test: TimeDepositCalculatorTest, PlanTypeTest                           │
     │ Type: Unit, unchanged                                                   │
     │ Proves: Domain behavior                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Test: TimeDepositPersistenceAdapterTest                                 │
     │ Type: @DataJpaTest + @AutoConfigureTestDatabase(replace = NONE) +       │
     │   Testcontainers PostgreSQL + Flyway;                                   │
     │   @Import(TimeDepositPersistenceAdapter)                                │
     │ Proves: The schema migrates and the quoted camelCase mapping            │
     │   validates; findAllWithWithdrawals returns deposits with their         │
     │   withdrawals (and [] when there are none) ordered by id; the money     │
     │   boundary: saving 1237654.3099999998 stores 1237654.31, and a          │
     │   NUMERIC value loads as the expected double; findAllForUpdate runs     │
     │   on PostgreSQL (FOR UPDATE + ORDER BY)                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Test: DemoDataMigrationTest                                             │
     │ Type: @DataJpaTest with db/migration + db/demo                          │
     │ Proves: The demo script applies and loads its rows (catches SQL         │
     │   mistakes that otherwise only show up at runtime)                      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Test: TimeDepositApiTest                                                │
     │ Type: @SpringBootTest(RANDOM_PORT) + Testcontainers + @Sql fixture;     │
     │   default profile, so no demo data                                      │
     │ Proves: POST → 204; GET → 200 with exact JSON: field names,             │
     │   withdrawals shape, balances 10008.33 / 10041.67 / unchanged for       │
     │   student day 366, premium day 45 and an unknown plan; the money        │
     │   boundary through the API (student 1234567.89 → 1237654.31); a         │
     │   second POST compounds (10016.67); two concurrent POSTs → both         │
     │   months applied; /v3/api-docs documents exactly the two API paths      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Shared container: one TestcontainersConfiguration with a single       │
     │   shared PostgreSQLContainer and @ServiceConnection, so all test        │
     │   contexts reuse the same container.                                    │
     │ - Honest caveat on the concurrency test: it checks the outcome. Without │
     │   the lock it would fail only when the race actually happens, so it     │
     │   guards the behavior rather than proving the lock. The lock itself is  │
     │   visible in one annotation, and the review covers it.                  │
     │ - Trade-off: the end-to-end test is slower and points less precisely at │
     │   the failing part than slice tests. At this size, real-stack coverage  │
     │   is worth more than mocked overlap.                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 8. Implementation sequence                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Each step is one commit, and mvn test stays green after each.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Boot skeleton, no database yet.                                      │
     │    - pom.xml: parent spring-boot-starter-parent:3.5.16,                 │
     │      spring-boot-starter-web, springdoc-openapi-starter-webmvc-ui (a    │
     │      2.x version compatible with Boot 3.5; confirmed by the step-4      │
     │      api-docs test), spring-boot-starter-test.                          │
     │    - Drop the explicit JUnit, AssertJ and surefire versions; the Boot   │
     │      BOM manages them.                                                  │
     │    - Add TimeDepositApplication and TimeDepositCalculator as a @Bean.   │
     │    - No JPA, Flyway or driver dependencies yet. They would trigger      │
     │      DataSource auto-configuration, which fails without a database URL. │
     │      No new test.                                                       │
     │    - Check: the 53 existing tests pass under the managed JUnit and      │
     │      AssertJ versions.                                                  │
     │ 2. Database and Testcontainers together.                                │
     │    - Add spring-boot-starter-data-jpa, flyway-core,                     │
     │      flyway-database-postgresql, postgresql (runtime);                  │
     │      spring-boot-testcontainers and org.testcontainers:postgresql       │
     │      (test).                                                            │
     │    - Add V1 schema, application.properties (datasource URL from         │
     │      environment variables, defaulting to the compose database),        │
     │      application-dev.properties, R__demo_data.sql, compose.yaml         │
     │      (PostgreSQL) and TestcontainersConfiguration.                      │
     │    - Check: DemoDataMigrationTest. This is the first test that needs    │
     │      PostgreSQL, and it exists together with the Testcontainers setup.  │
     │ 3. Persistence: domain Withdrawal; entities, TimeDepositJpaRepository,  │
     │    port, read record, adapter.                                          │
     │    - Check: TimeDepositPersistenceAdapterTest.                          │
     │ 4. Application and web: TimeDepositService (transactions, lock),        │
     │    controller, response records, OpenAPI annotations.                   │
     │    - Check: TimeDepositApiTest.                                         │
     │ 5. Documentation:                                                       │
     │    - README: run with docker compose up -d and mvn spring-boot:run      │
     │      -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=dev, Swagger UI "Try it out" steps for  │
     │      both endpoints, assumptions, the money boundary, concurrency vs.   │
     │      idempotency;                                                       │
     │    - update INITIAL_ANALYSIS.md and AI_USAGE.md;                        │
     │    - dependency and dead-code review.                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 9. Ambiguities to document                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Endpoints: POST /time-deposits/balance-updates → 204 and GET         │
     │    /time-deposits → 200. The README specifies neither verbs nor paths.  │
     │ 2. "Exactly two endpoints": springdoc's /v3/api-docs and /swagger-ui    │
     │    are documentation, required by the README's Swagger requirement, not │
     │    API endpoints.                                                       │
     │ 3. Idempotency: each POST applies another month, and days is unchanged. │
     │    Not idempotent, by design (legacy).                                  │
     │ 4. Concurrency: serialized by a pessimistic lock in deterministic id    │
     │    order. This is the one addition beyond the README, made for          │
     │    correctness.                                                         │
     │ 5. Money: NUMERIC(19,2); the stored value is rounded HALF_UP to two     │
     │    decimals and can differ from the calculator's raw double (§5.4).     │
     │ 6. Names: the README's camelCase table and column names are kept        │
     │    exactly, using quoted identifiers.                                   │
     │ 7. Plan types: not constrained in the database; unknown types are       │
     │    stored and earn 0.                                                   │
     │ 8. withdrawals: an array of {id, amount, date} ordered by id; [] when   │
     │    there are none; withdrawals don't affect interest.                   │
     │ 9. Order: deposits are returned ordered by id.                          │
     │ 10. IDs: plain INTEGER primary keys with no generation, because no      │
     │     endpoint creates records.                                           │
     │ 11. Demo data: loaded only with the dev profile; never in the default   │
     │     configuration or in tests.                                          │
     │ 12. days: out of scope. It is stored data, and nothing increments it.   │
     │ 13. Dates: LocalDate with no time zone, as ISO strings.                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verification                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - After every step:                                                     │
     │   - mvn test is green;                                                  │
     │   - git diff --quiet <step-base> --                                     │
     │     src/main/java/org/ikigaidigital/TimeDeposit.java                    │
     │     src/main/java/org/ikigaidigital/TimeDepositCalculator.java          │
     │     src/main/java/org/ikigaidigital/PlanType.java                       │
     │     src/test/java/org/ikigaidigital/TimeDepositCalculatorTest.java      │
     │     src/test/java/org/ikigaidigital/PlanTypeTest.java shows nothing     │
     │     changed.                                                            │
     │ - At the end: docker compose up -d && mvn spring-boot:run               │
     │   -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=dev, then in Swagger UI call GET → POST →  │
     │   GET and check the balances against the demo rows.                     │
     │ - Without the dev profile: GET returns [], which shows the demo data is │
     │   separate.                                                             │
     │ - Prerequisite: start Docker before step 2.                             │
     ╰─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯

❯ Revise the architecture plan to favor short-lived transactions and avoid      
  unnecessary application-level transactions for read-only operations.          
                                                                                
  Decisions:                                                                    
  - GET /time-deposits should not have an explicit application-service          
  @Transactional annotation. Load and map all required data within the          
  persistence adapter.                                                          
  - POST /time-deposits/balance-updates should use one short @Transactional     
  boundary covering load, interest calculation and persistence, to preserve     
  atomicity.                                                                    
  - No optimistic or pessimistic locking for now, because concurrency control   
  is outside the README requirements.                                           
  - Do not split the POST into read-outside-transaction and                     
  write-inside-transaction without explicitly explaining the stale-read and     
  lost-update risks.                                                            
  - Avoid Open Session in View; use spring.jpa.open-in-view=false.              
  - Keep the domain model and characterization tests unchanged.                 
                                                                                
  Update the architecture plan only. Do not modify application code yet.
