❯ Review the current PlanType enum, StandardInterestCalculation,                
  InterestCalculationStrategy, TimeDepositCalculator, and all existing tests.   
                                                                                
  I want to remove the coupling between the fixed PlanType enum and the         
  interest calculation rules to improve extensibility for future plan types and 
  calculation strategies.                                                       
                                                                                
  Goals:                                                                        
  1. Evaluate replacing the PlanType enum with data-driven interest policies    
  identified by the existing String planType code.                              
  2. Keep annual interest rates and eligibility windows in an InterestPolicy    
  value object.                                                                 
  3. Introduce the smallest practical policy lookup mechanism, avoiding         
  unnecessary registries or frameworks.                                         
  4. Keep StandardInterestCalculation focused on the existing monthly           
  calculation.                                                                  
  5. Preserve the ability to introduce completely different calculation         
  strategies in the future.                                                     
  6. Make new plan definitions possible without changing the core calculator or 
  the existing standard calculation algorithm.                                  
                                                                                
  Constraints:                                                                  
  - Preserve updateBalance(List<TimeDeposit>) and the existing TimeDeposit API. 
  - Preserve exact legacy calculation behavior for all existing valid inputs,   
  including unknown plan codes.                                                 
  - Preserve double arithmetic, operation order and new BigDecimal(double)      
  rounding.                                                                     
  - Keep characterization tests unchanged.                                      
  - Keep the domain framework-independent.                                      
  - Avoid adding one strategy or class per existing plan.                       
  - Do not introduce new calculation formulas or business rules.                
                                                                                
  Before implementation:                                                        
  - Compare the current enum-based solution against a data-driven policy-based  
  design.                                                                       
  - Identify whether an InterestPolicy record and a simple lookup mechanism are 
  sufficient.                                                                   
  - Explain how future plans with different formulas would be supported and     
  selected.                                                                     
  - Identify any unnecessary abstractions or limitations.                       
  - Propose the smallest practical refactoring plan.                            
                                                                                
  Do not modify any files yet. Wait for approval.

❯ I approve the proposed data-driven InterestPolicy refactoring with the        
  following adjustments:                                                        
                                                                                
  1. Rename LegacyInterestPolicies to StandardInterestPolicies. These are the   
  current default business rules, not obsolete rules.                           
  2. Prefer exposing the default immutable policy map through a defaults()      
  method rather than a public static POLICIES field.                            
  3. Keep the no-end-date sentinel encapsulated where practical. Avoid exposing 
  unnecessary implementation details.                                           
  4. Do not rely on Map.copyOf(...).get(null) throwing NullPointerException as  
  a compatibility guarantee. Invalid input handling is outside the README       
  requirements.                                                                 
                                                                                
  Implement the approved plan incrementally.                                    
                                                                                
  Preserve all valid-input behavior, the existing calculation formula,          
  floating-point operation order and rounding semantics.                        
  Keep TimeDeposit, TimeDepositCalculator, InterestCalculationStrategy, the     
  existing characterization tests, and the REST/persistence layers unchanged.   
                                                                                
  Add only the focused policy extension tests proposed in the plan. Remove      
  obsolete PlanType tests only because the corresponding enum no longer exists. 
                                                                                
  Run the complete Maven test suite, review the diff, update the architecture   
  and AI usage documentation, and report actual verification results.           
                                                                                
  Do not commit changes.

⏺ Approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: 
     ~/.claude/plans/pasted-content-id-9f1f-review-the-validated-iverson.md · 
     /plan to edit
     Plan: data-driven interest policies instead of the PlanType enum

     ▎ Approved with adjustments:
     ▎ 1. LegacyInterestPolicies → StandardInterestPolicies: these are the 
     ▎ current default rules, not obsolete ones.
     ▎ 2. The default map is exposed through 
     ▎ StandardInterestPolicies.defaults(), not a public static field.
     ▎ 3. The no-end-date sentinel is encapsulated: InterestPolicy becomes a 
     ▎ small final value class with private fields and two of(...) factories 
     ▎ (with and without a last interest day), exposing only annualRate() and 
     ▎ earnsInterestOn(days). A record would expose lastInterestDay() and the 
     ▎ Integer.MAX_VALUE sentinel through its accessor.
     ▎ 4. No reliance on Map.copyOf(...).get(null) throwing: null plan-type 
     ▎ behavior is unspecified, because invalid input is outside the README. 
     ▎ Map.copyOf is kept only for immutability. The docs record N2 as 
     ▎ unspecified.

     Context

     StandardInterestCalculation (now org.ikigaidigital.calculation.standard,
     uncommitted move) calls PlanType.fromCode(...) directly, so the set of
     plans is fixed at compile time inside a core domain enum. The developer
     wants plan definitions (rate + eligibility window) to be data keyed by the 
     existing String planType code. That way new plans can be defined without
     editing the calculator, the standard algorithm or an enum, while
     InterestCalculationStrategy stays available for genuinely different
     formulas.

     Valid-input behavior, rounding and the characterization tests must stay
     unchanged.

     Current state (read-only review):
     - PlanType (enum): code, annualRate, interestFreeDays, lastInterestDay
       (Integer.MAX_VALUE = no end), earnsInterestOn(days), fromCode(code)
       (exact match, case-sensitive, NullPointerException on null). It also
       carries the Javadoc justifying the 30/45/365 boundaries.
     - The only user of PlanType: StandardInterestCalculation.calculateInterest,
       via fromCode → filter(earnsInterestOn) → balance * annualRate / 12 → 
       orElse(0.0). Plus its own PlanTypeTest (10 tests).
     - Persistence, web and TimeDeposit already use String planType, so none of
       them changes.
     - The working tree has uncommitted developer changes: the package move and
       the Javadoc additions. The plan builds on that state.

     1. Enum vs. data-driven policies

     Adding a plan with the same formula
     PlanType enum (today): Edit the enum (a core file); recompile
     InterestPolicy record + Map<String, InterestPolicy>: Pass a map
       containing it to new StandardInterestCalculation(policies): no edit
       to the calculator, the algorithm or the default table
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Plan codes
     PlanType enum (today): Compile-time constants; typos only possible in
       the one enum
     InterestPolicy record + Map<String, InterestPolicy>: Strings defined
       once, in the default table; typos possible in that table or in custom
       maps (same exposure as today)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Exhaustive switch over plans
     PlanType enum (today): Possible (not used)
     InterestPolicy record + Map<String, InterestPolicy>: Not possible (not
       needed: no code switches on plan)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Different plan sets per context (tests, future configuration)
     PlanType enum (today): Impossible
     InterestPolicy record + Map<String, InterestPolicy>: Natural: just
       another map
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Unknown code
     PlanType enum (today): Optional.empty() → 0
     InterestPolicy record + Map<String, InterestPolicy>: map.get(code) ==
       null → 0

     Verdict: a record plus a Map is sufficient. The plans differ only in data,
     and an immutable map lookup by exact code is the smallest lookup mechanism:
     no registry class, no builder, no framework.

     Trade-off: the enum's compile-time safety for plan codes is lost. That
     safety was the original reason for the enum, so it should be said openly.
     It is mitigated because:
     - the codes are defined in exactly one default table;
     - the characterization tests pin every code;
     - nothing else in the code base refers to plan codes as constants.

     2. Design (smallest change set)

     All in org.ikigaidigital.calculation.standard (next to
     StandardInterestCalculation):

     New InterestPolicy record: replaces the enum's data role.
     public record InterestPolicy(double annualRate, int interestFreeDays, int
     lastInterestDay) {
         public static final int NO_LAST_INTEREST_DAY = Integer.MAX_VALUE;
         public boolean earnsInterestOn(int days) { return days >
     interestFreeDays && days <= lastInterestDay; } // moved verbatim
     }

     New LegacyInterestPolicies: the default plan table, as data only.
     public final class LegacyInterestPolicies {
         public static final Map<String, InterestPolicy> POLICIES = Map.of(
             "basic",   new InterestPolicy(0.01, 30, NO_LAST_INTEREST_DAY),
             "student", new InterestPolicy(0.03, 30, 365),
             "premium", new InterestPolicy(0.05, 45, NO_LAST_INTEREST_DAY));
         private LegacyInterestPolicies() {}
     }
     - It takes over PlanType's Javadoc justifying the README ambiguities:
       annual rates divided by 12, "after 30/45 days" meaning strictly greater,
       and "1 year" meaning 365 days.
     - The values are exactly the enum's values.

     Changed StandardInterestCalculation: same algorithm; it only gets its
     policies from a map.
     - public StandardInterestCalculation() →
       this(LegacyInterestPolicies.POLICIES), so new TimeDepositCalculator()
       keeps working unchanged.
     - public StandardInterestCalculation(Map<String, InterestPolicy> 
       policiesByPlanType) → Map.copyOf(...): an immutable defensive copy that
       rejects null keys and values.
     - calculateInterest:
       Optional.ofNullable(policies.get(deposit.getPlanType())).filter(p -> 
       p.earnsInterestOn(days)).map(p -> monthlyInterest(balance, 
       p.annualRate())).orElse(0.0).
     - monthlyInterest(balance, annualRate) and its operation-order Javadoc stay
       unchanged.

     Removed: PlanType.java and PlanTypeTest.java. Nothing else references them.

     Unchanged:
     - TimeDeposit, TimeDepositCalculator (including new BigDecimal(double) /
       HALF_UP rounding), InterestCalculationStrategy;
     - TimeDepositApplication (new TimeDepositCalculator());
     - persistence, web, Flyway and demo data;
     - TimeDepositCalculatorTest;
     - TimeDepositCalculatorStrategyTest, which compiles as is because new 
       StandardInterestCalculation() still exists.

     Behavior details preserved:
     - Exact, case-sensitive match: String.equals / hashCode, the same as
       fromCode.
     - Unknown code: get returns null → Optional.empty() → 0.0.
     - null plan type: an immutable Map.copyOf map throws NullPointerException
       on get(null), the same as today's fromCode(null) (the accepted N2
       behavior).
     - Arithmetic: the same double values (0.01, 0.03, 0.05) and the same
       balance * annualRate / 12 expression, so results are bit-identical.

     3. Different formulas in the future: how they are supported and selected

     - Same formula, new plan: add an InterestPolicy entry to the map given to
       StandardInterestCalculation (or to LegacyInterestPolicies, if it becomes
       a default).
     - New formula (tiers, daily accrual): a new InterestCalculationStrategy
       implementation, passed to new TimeDepositCalculator(strategy).
       - Choosing a formula per plan: that strategy handles its own plan codes
         and passes everything else to StandardInterestCalculation, the
         composition already shown in TimeDepositCalculatorStrategyTest.
       - If several formulas ever need to be chosen per plan, a small
         Map<String, InterestCalculationStrategy> dispatching strategy can be
         added then. Not now (YAGNI).
     - Externally configured plans (e.g. bound from application.yml in the
       Spring configuration and passed to new StandardInterestCalculation(map)):
       this becomes possible without any domain change. Not implemented,
       because the README asks for no new behavior.

     Limits:
     - one strategy per calculator;
     - rules can't see withdrawals (the TimeDeposit API is frozen);
     - policies are only as consistent as the map supplied (no validation added,
       since the README says invalid input needn't be handled).

     4. Tests

     - Unchanged and must pass:
       - TimeDepositCalculatorTest (43): covers every boundary, every rate,
         unknown and wrongly cased codes, rounding;
       - TimeDepositCalculatorStrategyTest (2);
       - persistence, API and demo migration (8).
     - Removed: PlanTypeTest (10). It tests the deleted enum. Its lookup cases

⏺ I replaced the PlanType enum with data-driven interest policies, in three
  steps. All 55 tests pass and valid-input behavior is unchanged. Nothing is
  committed.

  What changed (org.ikigaidigital.calculation.standard):
  - InterestPolicy (new):
    - a small final value class holding a plan's annual rate and the range of
      days that earn interest;
    - created through two factories, of(rate, interestFreeDays) and of(rate, 
      interestFreeDays, lastInterestDay);
    - public methods are only annualRate() and earnsInterestOn(days), and the
      comparison logic is unchanged from the enum;
    - the Integer.MAX_VALUE meaning "no end date" stays private. I used a class
      instead of a record because a record would expose it through
      lastInterestDay().
  - StandardInterestPolicies (new):
    - defaults() returns an immutable map of the basic, student and premium
      terms, with exactly the enum's values;
    - plans are found by their exact, case-sensitive code;
    - the Javadoc explaining the README's ambiguous rules (rate / 12, "after N
      days" meaning strictly after, 1 year = 365 days) moved here from PlanType.
  - StandardInterestCalculation:
    - a new constructor takes a policy map (copied with Map.copyOf, for
      immutability only);
    - the no-argument constructor uses defaults();
    - the lookup is Optional.ofNullable(map.get(code)), and an unknown code
      still gives 0;
    - monthlyInterest(balance, annualRate) and its operation order are
      unchanged.
  - Removed: PlanType and PlanTypeTest (10 tests), since the enum no longer
    exists. A whole-word search finds no remaining references in src. I deleted
    them with git rm, so the two deletions are already staged; everything else
    is unstaged.

  New StandardInterestCalculationTest (2 tests, no mocks):
  1. A new plan as data: a test-only "gold" plan (2%) added to a copy of
     defaults(). Gold → 10016.67, and basic still → 10008.33.
  2. The map decides which plans exist: with a map containing only "basic", a
     premium deposit at day 46 stays 10000.00 while basic earns its interest.

  Both would fail if the calculation ignored the map it's given. "Gold" appears
  nowhere in the production code.

  Verification:
  - Tests: 55 run, 0 failures: 43 characterization, 2 strategy, 2 standard
    policy, 3 persistence, 4 API, 1 demo migration.
  - Unchanged files (git diff): the characterization tests, TimeDeposit, the
    application, persistence and web layers, and the database scripts.
  - Public API (javap): TimeDeposit, TimeDepositCalculator and
    InterestCalculationStrategy are identical to before.
  - Comparison with the original calculator: 0 differences over 2,000,000 random
    deposits, including unknown codes.
  - null plan type: it currently still throws NullPointerException. As you
    asked, the docs now call this unspecified rather than guaranteed.

  Docs:
  - INITIAL_ANALYSIS.md:
    - new step 10, and step 9 marked done (33d853e);
    - step 3 marked as replaced;
    - architecture tree and decisions updated;
    - N2 now says null behavior is unspecified;
    - records the trade-off: plan codes are no longer checked at compile time.
      That was the original reason for the enum; it's mitigated by keeping the
      codes in one default table that the tests cover.
  - AI_USAGE.md: a workflow row, a new §3.8, a decisions row (the enum row
    marked as superseded) and the new test counts.
  - java/README.md: architecture and "interest rules" updated (a new plan is a
    new map entry), plus the new test row. It no longer mentions PlanType.
  - Root README.md: untouched.
